Flow_Table TABLE

Configuration for a particular OpenFlow table.

Summary:

name
optional string
Eviction Policy:
flow_limit
optional integer, at least 0
overflow_policy
optional string, either evict or refuse
groups
set of strings
Classifier Optimization:
prefixes
set of up to 4 strings
Common Columns:
external_ids
map of string-string pairs

Details:

name: optional string
The table’s name. Set this column to change the name that controllers will receive when they request table statistics, e.g. ovs-ofctl dump-tables. The name does not affect switch behavior.
Eviction Policy:

Open vSwitch supports limiting the number of flows that may be installed in a flow table, via the flow_limit column. When adding a flow would exceed this limit, by default Open vSwitch reports an error, but there are two ways to configure Open vSwitch to instead delete (``evict’’) a flow to make room for the new one:

  • Set the overflow_policy column to evict.
  • Send an OpenFlow 1.4+ ``table mod request’’ to enable eviction for the flow table (e.g. ovs-ofctl -O OpenFlow14 mod-table br0 0 evict to enable eviction on flow table 0 of bridge br0).

When a flow must be evicted due to overflow, the flow to evict is chosen through an approximation of the following algorithm. This algorithm is used regardless of how eviction was enabled:

1.
Divide the flows in the table into groups based on the values of the fields or sub-fields specified in the groups column, so that all of the flows in a given group have the same values for those fields. If a flow does not specify a given field, that field’s value is treated as 0. If groups is empty, then all of the flows in the flow table are treated as a single group.
2.
Consider the flows in the largest group, that is, the group that contains the greatest number of flows. If two or more groups all have the same largest number of flows, consider the flows in all of those groups.
3.
If the flows under consideration have different importance values, eliminate from consideration any flows except those with the lowest importance. (``Importance,’’ a 16-bit integer value attached to each flow, was introduced in OpenFlow 1.4. Flows inserted with older versions of OpenFlow always have an importance of 0.)
4.
Among the flows under consideration, choose the flow that expires soonest for eviction.

The eviction process only considers flows that have an idle timeout or a hard timeout. That is, eviction never deletes permanent flows. (Permanent flows do count against flow_limit.)

flow_limit: optional integer, at least 0
If set, limits the number of flows that may be added to the table. Open vSwitch may limit the number of flows in a table for other reasons, e.g. due to hardware limitations or for resource availability or performance reasons.
overflow_policy: optional string, either evict or refuse
Controls the switch’s behavior when an OpenFlow flow table modification request would add flows in excess of flow_limit. The supported values are:
refuse
Refuse to add the flow or flows. This is also the default policy when overflow_policy is unset.
evict
Delete a flow chosen according to the algorithm described above.
groups: set of strings
When overflow_policy is evict, this controls how flows are chosen for eviction when the flow table would otherwise exceed flow_limit flows. Its value is a set of NXM fields or sub-fields, each of which takes one of the forms field[] or field[start..end], e.g. NXM_OF_IN_PORT[]. Please see meta-flow.h for a complete list of NXM field names.
Open vSwitch ignores any invalid or unknown field specifications.
When eviction is not enabled, via overflow_policy or an OpenFlow 1.4+ ``table mod,’’ this column has no effect.
Classifier Optimization:
prefixes: set of up to 4 strings
This string set specifies which fields should be used for address prefix tracking. Prefix tracking allows the classifier to skip rules with longer than necessary prefixes, resulting in better wildcarding for datapath flows.
Prefix tracking may be beneficial when a flow table contains matches on IP address fields with different prefix lengths. For example, when a flow table contains IP address matches on both full addresses and proper prefixes, the full address matches will typically cause the datapath flow to un-wildcard the whole address field (depending on flow entry priorities). In this case each packet with a different address gets handed to the userspace for flow processing and generates its own datapath flow. With prefix tracking enabled for the address field in question packets with addresses matching shorter prefixes would generate datapath flows where the irrelevant address bits are wildcarded, allowing the same datapath flow to handle all the packets within the prefix in question. In this case many userspace upcalls can be avoided and the overall performance can be better.
This is a performance optimization only, so packets will receive the same treatment with or without prefix tracking.
The supported fields are: tun_id, tun_src, tun_dst, tun_ipv6_src, tun_ipv6_dst, nw_src, nw_dst (or aliases ip_src and ip_dst), ipv6_src, and ipv6_dst. (Using this feature for tun_id would only make sense if the tunnel IDs have prefix structure similar to IP addresses.)
By default, the prefixes=ip_dst,ip_src,ipv6_dst,ipv6_src are used on each flow table. This instructs the flow classifier to track the IPv4 and IPv6 destination and source addresses used by the rules in this specific flow table.
The keyword none is recognized as an explicit override of the default values, causing no prefix fields to be tracked.
To set the prefix fields, the flow table record needs to exist:
ovs-vsctl set Bridge br0 flow_tables:0=@N1 -- --id=@N1 create Flow_Table name=table0
Creates a flow table record for the OpenFlow table number 0.
ovs-vsctl set Flow_Table table0 prefixes=ip_dst,ip_src
Enables prefix tracking for IPv4 source and destination address fields.
There is a maximum number of fields that can be enabled for any one flow table. Currently this limit is 4.
Common Columns:

The overall purpose of these columns is described under Common Columns at the beginning of this document.

external_ids: map of string-string pairs

  • lb-fields: A comma-separated list of fields to use for load balancing NetFlow records across multiple collectors. This is only used when multiple collectors are specified in targets.

The OpenFlow controller's address. This can be a TCP address (e.g. tcp:192.168.0.1:6633) or a local Unix socket (e.g. unix:/var/run/openvswitch/control.sock).
connection_max_backoff: optional integer
The maximum amount of time (in milliseconds) to wait between connection attempts to the controller. Defaults to 2000.
max_backoff: optional integer
The maximum amount of time (in milliseconds) to wait between connection attempts to the controller. This is deprecated and should not be used.

If set to true, OpenFlow messages will be sent over the OpenFlow protocol itself. This requires that the controller is reachable via the OpenFlow bridge. If set to false, OpenFlow messages will be sent over a separate out-of-band channel. This is the default.

  • rate-limit: The maximum rate (in packets per second) at which OpenFlow messages can be sent to the controller. Defaults to 0 (unlimited).

  • burst-limit: The maximum burst size (in packets) for OpenFlow messages. Defaults to 0 (unlimited).

  • probe-interval: The interval (in milliseconds) at which to send echo requests to the controller. Defaults to 5000.

  • failure-domain: A string used to group controllers that share a common failure domain. If a controller in a given domain fails, Open vSwitch will attempt to connect to other controllers in the same domain.

System TABLE

System-wide settings.

Summary:

other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
Common Columns:
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

other_config: optional map of string-string pairs

  • datapath-id: The datapath ID of the switch. This is a 64-bit integer that uniquely identifies the switch. If this is unset, then the datapath ID will be auto-generated.

  • hw-addr: The MAC address of the switch. This is used as the datapath ID if the datapath ID is not specified.

  • hw-switch: The type of hardware switch. This is used to configure specific hardware features.

  • hw-mac-table-size: The size of the MAC address table in the hardware switch.

  • hw-flow-table-size: The size of the flow table in the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-ports: The maximum number of ports supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-queues: The maximum number of queues supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-mirrors: The maximum number of mirror ports supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-vlans: The maximum number of VLANs supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-rules: The maximum number of rules supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-table-miss-behavior: The behavior of the hardware switch when a flow is not found in the flow table. Possible values are drop, normal, and controller.

  • hw-table-miss-action: The action to take when a flow is not found in the flow table. This can be a drop, normal, or controller action.

  • hw-flow-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which flow statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-port-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which port statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-queue-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which queue statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-mirror-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which mirror statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-vlan-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which VLAN statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-rule-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which rule statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-arp-trap-priority: The priority of ARP trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-nd-trap-priority: The priority of Neighbor Discovery trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-ipv6-ra-trap-priority: The priority of IPv6 Router Advertisement trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-dhcp-trap-priority: The priority of DHCP trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-dhcpv6-trap-priority: The priority of DHCPv6 trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

The name of the bridge to send IPFIX data for. If this is unset, then IPFIX data will be collected for all bridges.
cache_entries: optional integer
The maximum number of IPFIX cache entries. If this is unset, a default value will be used.
cache_active_timeout: optional integer
The interval (in seconds) at which active IPFIX cache entries will be reported. If this is 0, then active entries will only be reported when they are timed out. Default is 300 seconds.
cache_inactive_timeout: optional integer
The interval (in seconds) at which inactive IPFIX cache entries will be reported. If this is 0, then inactive entries will never be reported. Default is 60 seconds.
subagent: optional integer
The IPFIX sub-agent ID. If this is unset, then the sub-agent ID will be auto-generated.
obs-domain-id: optional integer
The IPFIX Observation Domain ID. If this is unset, then the Observation Domain ID will be auto-generated.
obs-point-type: optional string, either interface or switch
The type of observation point. If interface, then the observation point is an interface. If switch, then the observation point is the switch.
other_config: optional map of string-string pairs

  • sampling-rate: The rate at which packets are sampled for IPFIX. For example, a value of 100 means that 1 out of every 100 packets will be sampled.

  • ingress-only: If set to true, only ingress traffic will be sent to the IPFIX collector. Defaults to false.

  • lb-fields: A comma-separated list of fields to use for load balancing IPFIX records across multiple collectors. This is only used when multiple collectors are specified in targets.

external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

NAT TABLE

Summary:

type
required string, either dnat or snat
external_ip
required string
optional integer

optional integer
optional string
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

type: required string, either dnat or snat
The type of NAT to perform. dnat is for destination NAT (i.e., changing the destination IP address). snat is for source NAT (i.e., changing the source IP address).
external_ip: required string
The external IP address. For DNAT, this is the IP address that traffic will be sent to. For SNAT, this is the IP address that traffic will be sent from.
external_netherlands: optional integer
The external port number. This is only used for SNAT and only when the protocol is TCP or UDP.
internal_ip: optional string
The internal IP address. For DNAT, this is the IP address that traffic will be translated to. For SNAT, this is the IP address that traffic will be translated from.
internal_netherlands: optional integer
The internal port number. This is only used for SNAT and only when the protocol is TCP or UDP.
logical_bridge: optional string
The name of the logical bridge to which this NAT rule applies. If this is unset, the NAT rule applies to all bridges.
other_config: optional map of string-string pairs

  • protocol: The protocol for which this NAT rule applies. This can be tcp, udp, or all. The default is all.

external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

qos
required string

required map of integer-struct

other_config
optional map of string-string pairs

The name of the QoS configuration to update.

A map from queue ID to queue configuration. The queue configuration struct contains the following fields:
min_rate
optional integer, at least 0
max_rate
optional integer, at least 0
other_config: optional map of string-string pairs

  • min-rate: The minimum rate (in kbps) for this queue. This is a guaranteed rate. If this is set, then packets in this queue will be dequeued at at least this rate.

  • max-rate: The maximum rate (in kbps) for this queue. If this is set, then packets in this queue will be dequeued at at most this rate.

  • burst: The burst size (in kbps) for this queue. This is the maximum amount of data that can be sent in a short period of time. If this is set, then packets in this queue will be dequeued at most this rate for this duration.

Bridge OPTIONS

Open vSwitch bridge options.

Summary:

options
optional map of string-string pairs
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

options: optional map of string-string pairs

  • datapath-id: The datapath ID of the bridge. This is a 64-bit integer that uniquely identifies the bridge. If this is unset, then the datapath ID will be auto-generated.

  • hw-addr: The MAC address of the bridge. This is used as the datapath ID if the datapath ID is not specified.

  • hw-switch: The type of hardware switch. This is used to configure specific hardware features.

  • hw-mac-table-size: The size of the MAC address table in the hardware switch.

  • hw-flow-table-size: The size of the flow table in the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-ports: The maximum number of ports supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-queues: The maximum number of queues supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-mirrors: The maximum number of mirror ports supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-vlans: The maximum number of VLANs supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-rules: The maximum number of rules supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-table-miss-behavior: The behavior of the hardware switch when a flow is not found in the flow table. Possible values are drop, normal, and controller.

  • hw-table-miss-action: The action to take when a flow is not found in the flow table. This can be a drop, normal, or controller action.

  • hw-flow-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which flow statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-port-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which port statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-queue-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which queue statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-mirror-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which mirror statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-vlan-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which VLAN statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-rule-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which rule statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-arp-trap-priority: The priority of ARP trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-nd-trap-priority: The priority of Neighbor Discovery trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-ipv6-ra-trap-priority: The priority of IPv6 Router Advertisement trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-dhcp-trap-priority: The priority of DHCP trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-dhcpv6-trap-priority: The priority of DHCPv6 trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

other_config: optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

Manager TABLE

OpenFlow manager configuration.

Summary:

target
required string
connection_max_backoff
optional integer
max_backoff
optional integer
in-band
optional boolean
local_ip
optional string
local_netherlands
optional string
bridge
optional string
ssl_ca_cert
optional string
ssl_private_key
optional string
ssl_certificate
optional string
ssl_protocols
optional string
ssl_v2_ciphers
optional string
disabled_protocols
optional string
optional map of string-string pairs, read only
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

target: required string
The OpenFlow manager's address. This can be a TCP address (e.g. tcp:192.168.0.1:6633) or a local Unix socket (e.g. unix:/var/run/openvswitch/control.sock).
connection_max_backoff: optional integer
The maximum amount of time (in milliseconds) to wait between connection attempts to the manager. Defaults to 2000.
max_backoff: optional integer
The maximum amount of time (in milliseconds) to wait between connection attempts to the manager. This is deprecated and should not be used.
in-band: optional boolean
If set to true, OpenFlow messages will be sent over the OpenFlow protocol itself. This requires that the manager is reachable via the OpenFlow bridge. If set to false, OpenFlow messages will be sent over a separate out-of-band channel. This is the default.
local_ip: optional string
The IP address to use as the source IP address for connections to the manager. This is useful when the switch has multiple IP addresses.
local_netherlands: optional string
The TCP/IP port number to use as the source port number for connections to the manager. This is useful when the switch has multiple IP addresses.
bridge: optional string
The name of the bridge to which this manager should connect. If not specified, the manager will connect to all bridges.
ssl_ca_cert: optional string
The CA certificate file to use for SSL connections to the manager. This is used to verify the manager's certificate.
ssl_private_key: optional string
The private key file to use for SSL connections to the manager. This is used to authenticate the switch to the manager.
ssl_certificate: optional string
The certificate file to use for SSL connections to the manager. This is used to authenticate the switch to the manager.
ssl_protocols: optional string
A comma-separated list of SSL protocols to enable. Supported protocols are TLSv1.0, TLSv1.1, and TLSv1.2. If not specified, all supported protocols are enabled.
ssl_v2_ciphers: optional string
A comma-separated list of SSL ciphers to enable for SSLv3 and TLSv1.0 connections. If not specified, a default list of ciphers is used.
disabled_protocols: optional string
A comma-separated list of SSL protocols to disable. Supported protocols are TLSv1.0, TLSv1.1, and TLSv1.2. If not specified, no protocols are disabled.
status: optional map of string-string pairs, read only
The status of the connection to the manager. This map is read-only and contains information about the connection state, such as whether the manager is connected, the number of packets sent and received, and any error messages.
other_config: optional map of string-string pairs

  • rate-limit: The maximum rate (in packets per second) at which OpenFlow messages can be sent to the manager. Defaults to 0 (unlimited).

  • burst-limit: The maximum burst size (in packets) for OpenFlow messages. Defaults to 0 (unlimited).

  • probe-interval: The interval (in milliseconds) at which to send echo requests to the manager. Defaults to 5000.

  • failure-domain: A string used to group managers that share a common failure domain. If a manager in a given domain fails, Open vSwitch will attempt to connect to other managers in the same domain.

external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

Interface TABLE

Interface configuration.

Summary:

name
required string, immutable
type
optional string
options
optional map of string-string pairs
mtu
optional integer
mtu_request
optional integer
ingress_policing_rate
optional integer
ingress_policing_burst
optional integer
egress_policing_rate
optional integer
egress_policing_burst
optional integer
mac
optional string
up
optional boolean
admin_down
optional boolean
optional string, either disabled, active, or standby
optional map of string-string pairs, read only
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

name: required string
The name of the interface. This name must be unique within the bridge.
type: optional string
The type of interface. Possible values include:

  • system: A physical network interface.

  • patch: A patch port, used to connect bridges together.

  • internal: An internal interface, used for management or other purposes.

  • tap: A TAP interface, used to connect to a virtual machine.

  • geneve: A Geneve tunnel interface.

  • gre: A GRE tunnel interface.

  • vxlan: A VXLAN tunnel interface.

  • ipsec: An IPsec tunnel interface.

If not specified, the type is inferred from the name.
options: optional map of string-string pairs
Interface-specific options. See the Open vSwitch documentation for details on the options available for each interface type.
mtu: optional integer
The maximum transmission unit (MTU) for the interface. This is the maximum size of a packet that can be transmitted over the interface, in bytes. If not specified, the default MTU for the interface type is used.
mtu_request: optional integer
The MTU requested by the interface. This is used for negotiating the MTU with remote devices. If not specified, the value of mtu is used.
ingress_policing_rate: optional integer
The ingress policing rate in bits per second. If this is set, then packets arriving on this interface will be policed to this rate. If this is 0, then ingress policing is disabled.
ingress_policing_burst: optional integer
The ingress policing burst size in bits. If this is set, then packets arriving on this interface will be allowed to burst up to this size. If this is 0, then ingress policing is disabled.
egress_policing_rate: optional integer
The egress policing rate in bits per second. If this is set, then packets leaving this interface will be policed to this rate. If this is 0, then egress policing is disabled.
egress_policing_burst: optional integer
The egress policing burst size in bits. If this is set, then packets leaving this interface will be allowed to burst up to this size. If this is 0, then egress policing is disabled.
mac: optional string
The MAC address of the interface. If not specified, a MAC address will be auto-generated.
up: optional boolean
If set to true, the interface is enabled. If set to false, the interface is disabled.
admin_down: optional boolean
If set to true, the interface is administratively down. This is equivalent to setting up to false.
link_resilience: optional string, either disabled, active, or standby
Controls the link resilience behavior of the interface.

  • disabled: Link resilience is disabled.

  • active: The interface is active and will attempt to maintain link.

  • standby: The interface is in standby mode and will only become active if the primary interface fails.

Defaults to disabled.
status: optional map of string-string pairs, read only
The status of the interface. This map is read-only and contains information about the interface's current state, such as its link status, speed, and duplex mode.
other_config: optional map of string-string pairs

  • hwaddr: The MAC address of the interface. This is useful for configuring interfaces that do not have a MAC address assigned automatically.

  • peer: The name of the peer interface for a patch port.

  • remote_ip: The remote IP address for a tunnel interface.

  • remote_net: The remote network address for a tunnel interface.

  • local_ip: The local IP address for a tunnel interface.

  • local_net: The local network address for a tunnel interface.

  • key: The key for a tunnel interface. This is used to identify the tunnel.

  • vxlan-id: The VXLAN network identifier.

  • geneve-id: The Geneve network identifier.

  • vlan-id: The VLAN ID for a VLAN interface.

  • vlan-mode: The VLAN mode for a VLAN interface. Possible values are access, tunnel, native-ctag, and native-utag.

  • trunks: A space-separated list of VLAN IDs that are allowed to trunk over this interface.

  • rx-vlan-forty-seven: If set to true, permit VLAN IDs up to 4095. Otherwise, permit only VLAN IDs up to 4094.

  • bpdu-guard: If set to true, the interface will be disabled if it receives a BPDU. This is a security feature to prevent loops.

  • rstp-guard: If set to true, the interface will be disabled if it receives a BPDU that indicates RSTP is enabled on the connected device. This is a security feature to prevent loops.

  • dhcp-vlan: If set to true, the interface will be treated as a DHCP relay interface. This means that DHCP packets will be allowed to pass through even if they are otherwise blocked by VLAN filtering.

external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

Port_SECURITY TABLE

Port security configuration.

Summary:

name
required string, immutable
allow_unspecified
optional boolean
allow_labeled
optional boolean
allow_unlabeled
optional boolean
mac_learning
optional string, either disabled, active, or inactive
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

name: required string
The name of the port security policy. This name must be unique within the bridge.
allow_unspecified: optional boolean
If set to true, the port will allow traffic from unspecified MAC addresses. Unspecified MAC addresses are those that are not explicitly listed in the Port's MAC address table. This is useful for allowing new devices to connect to the network. The default is false.
allow_labeled: optional boolean
If set to true, the port will allow traffic with VLAN tags. This is useful for networks that use VLANs. The default is false.
allow_unlabeled: optional boolean
If set to true, the port will allow traffic without VLAN tags. This is useful for networks that do not use VLANs. The default is false.
mac_learning: optional string, either disabled, active, or inactive
Controls MAC learning on the port.

  • disabled: MAC learning is disabled. The switch will not learn any MAC addresses on this port.

  • active: MAC learning is enabled. The switch will learn MAC addresses on this port and add them to its MAC address table.

  • inactive: MAC learning is enabled, but the switch will not learn any MAC addresses on this port. This is useful for ports that are connected to devices that do not need to be learned, such as routers or firewalls.

The default is active.
other_config: optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

Connection TABLE

OpenFlow connection configuration.

Summary:

target
required string
optional integer
max_backoff
optional integer
in-band
optional boolean
local_ip
optional string
local_netherlands
optional string
bridge
optional string
ssl_ca_cert
optional string
ssl_private_key
optional string
ssl_certificate
optional string
ssl_protocols
optional string
ssl_v2_ciphers
optional string
disabled_protocols
optional string
optional map of string-string pairs, read only
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

target: required string
The OpenFlow controller's address. This can be a TCP address (e.g. tcp:192.168.0.1:6633) or a local Unix socket (e.g. unix:/var/run/openvswitch/control.sock).
inactivity_probe: optional integer
The maximum amount of time (in milliseconds) to wait without receiving a message from the controller before sending an echo request. Defaults to 5000.
max_backoff: optional integer
The maximum amount of time (in milliseconds) to wait between connection attempts to the controller. Defaults to 2000.
in-band: optional boolean
If set to true, OpenFlow messages will be sent over the OpenFlow protocol itself. This requires that the controller is reachable via the OpenFlow bridge. If set to false, OpenFlow messages will be sent over a separate out-of-band channel. This is the default.
local_ip: optional string
The IP address to use as the source IP address for connections to the controller. This is useful when the switch has multiple IP addresses.
local_netherlands: optional string
The TCP/IP port number to use as the source port number for connections to the controller. This is useful when the switch has multiple IP addresses.
bridge: optional string
The name of the bridge to which this connection should be established. If not specified, the connection will be established to all bridges.
ssl_ca_cert: optional string
The CA certificate file to use for SSL connections to the controller. This is used to verify the controller's certificate.
ssl_private_key: optional string
The private key file to use for SSL connections to the controller. This is used to authenticate the switch to the controller.
ssl_certificate: optional string
The certificate file to use for SSL connections to the controller. This is used to authenticate the switch to the controller.
ssl_protocols: optional string
A comma-separated list of SSL protocols to enable. Supported protocols are TLSv1.0, TLSv1.1, and TLSv1.2. If not specified, all supported protocols are enabled.
ssl_v2_ciphers: optional string
A comma-separated list of SSL ciphers to enable for SSLv3 and TLSv1.0 connections. If not specified, a default list of ciphers is used.
disabled_protocols: optional string
A comma-separated list of SSL protocols to disable. Supported protocols are TLSv1.0, TLSv1.1, and TLSv1.2. If not specified, no protocols are disabled.
status: optional map of string-string pairs, read only
The status of the connection to the controller. This map is read-only and contains information about the connection state, such as whether the controller is connected, the number of packets sent and received, and any error messages.
other_config: optional map of string-string pairs

  • rate-limit: The maximum rate (in packets per second) at which OpenFlow messages can be sent to the controller. Defaults to 0 (unlimited).

  • burst-limit: The maximum burst size (in packets) for OpenFlow messages. Defaults to 0 (unlimited).

HA TABLE

High Availability (HA) configuration.

Summary:

node_id
required string, immutable
required string, immutable
required string
optional integer
optional string, either active-backup or failover
optional string, either active-backup or failover
priority
optional integer
optional integer
disabled
optional boolean
optional string, read only
optional map of string-string pairs, read only
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

node_id: required string
The ID of this HA node.
other_node_id: required string
The ID of the other HA node.
address: required string
The IP address and port of the other HA node. Format: IP:Port.
peer_port: optional integer
The port number on the peer node that is used for HA communication. If not specified, the default port is used.
method: optional string, either active-backup or failover
The HA method to use. active-backup means that one node is active and the other is in backup. failover means that the nodes will failover to each other if one fails.
mode: optional string, either active-backup or failover
The HA mode to use. active-backup means that one node is active and the other is in backup. failover means that the nodes will failover to each other if one fails.
priority: optional integer
The priority of this HA node. Lower values have higher priority. Defaults to 100.

The initial priority of this HA node. This is used when the node first joins the HA cluster. Defaults to 100.
disabled: optional boolean
If set to true, HA is disabled for this node. Defaults to false.
state: optional string, read only
The current state of the HA node. Possible values are master, backup, and standalone.

The status of the HA configuration. This map is read-only and contains information about the HA state, such as whether the cluster is healthy, the priority of each node, and any error messages.
other_config: optional map of string-string pairs

  • heartbeat-interval: The interval (in milliseconds) between heartbeats. Defaults to 1000.

  • heartbeat-timeout: The maximum time (in milliseconds) without receiving a heartbeat before declaring the other node down. Defaults to 3000.

NetFlow OPTIONS

NetFlow options.

Summary:

options
optional map of string-string pairs
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

options: optional map of string-string pairs

  • targets: A set of target collector addresses. Each address should be in the format IP:Port.

  • bridge: The name of the bridge to send NetFlow data for. If this is unset, then NetFlow data will be collected for all bridges.

  • engine-id: The NetFlow engine ID. If this is unset, then the engine ID will be auto-generated.

  • engine-boots: The number of times the NetFlow engine has been booted. If this is unset, then it will be initialized to 1.

  • active-timeout: The interval (in seconds) at which active flows will be reported. If this is 0, then active flows will only be reported when they are timed out. Default is 300 seconds.

  • records-per-packet: The maximum number of NetFlow records to send in a single packet. If this is unset, a default value will be used.

  • sampling-rate: The rate at which packets are sampled for NetFlow. For example, a value of 100 means that 1 out of every 100 packets will be sampled.

  • version: The NetFlow version to use. Supported values are 5, 9, and 10 (IPFIX). Defaults to 9.

  • ingress-only: If set to true, only ingress traffic will be sent to the NetFlow collector. Defaults to false.

  • lb-fields: A comma-separated list of fields to use for load balancing NetFlow records across multiple collectors. This is only used when multiple collectors are specified in targets.

other_config: optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

sFlow OPTIONS

sFlow options.

Summary:

options
optional map of string-string pairs
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

options: optional map of string-string pairs

  • agent: The IP address of the agent collecting sFlow data. If this is unset, then the agent IP address will be auto-generated.

  • targets: A set of target collector addresses. Each address should be in the format IP:Port.

  • header-size: The amount of header data (in bytes) to send for each sampled packet. The default is 128 bytes.

  • sampling-rate: The rate at which packets are sampled. For example, a value of 100 means that 1 out of every 100 packets will be sampled. The default is 100.

  • polling-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which interface counters are polled. The default is 20 seconds.

  • sub-agent: The sub-agent identifier for the sFlow agent. If this is unset, then the sub-agent ID will be auto-generated.

other_config: optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

Controller OPTIONS

Controller options.

Summary:

options
optional map of string-string pairs
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

options: optional map of string-string pairs

  • target: The OpenFlow controller's address. This can be a TCP address (e.g. tcp:192.168.0.1:6633) or a local Unix socket (e.g. unix:/var/run/openvswitch/control.sock).

  • connection-max-backoff: The maximum amount of time (in milliseconds) to wait between connection attempts to the controller. Defaults to 2000.

  • in-band: If set to true, OpenFlow messages will be sent over the OpenFlow protocol itself. This requires that the controller is reachable via the OpenFlow bridge. If set to false, OpenFlow messages will be sent over a separate out-of-band channel. This is the default.

  • local-ip: The IP address to use as the source IP address for connections to the controller. This is useful when the switch has multiple IP addresses.

  • local-netherlands: The TCP/IP port number to use as the source port number for connections to the controller. This is useful when the switch has multiple IP addresses.

  • bridge: The name of the bridge to which this controller should connect. If not specified, the controller will connect to all bridges.

  • ssl-ca-cert: The CA certificate file to use for SSL connections to the controller. This is used to verify the controller's certificate.

  • ssl-private-key: The private key file to use for SSL connections to the controller. This is used to authenticate the switch to the controller.

  • ssl-certificate: The certificate file to use for SSL connections to the controller. This is used to authenticate the switch to the controller.

  • ssl-protocols: A comma-separated list of SSL protocols to enable. Supported protocols are TLSv1.0, TLSv1.1, and TLSv1.2. If not specified, all supported protocols are enabled.

  • ssl-v2-ciphers: A comma-separated list of SSL ciphers to enable for SSLv3 and TLSv1.0 connections. If not specified, a default list of ciphers is used.

  • disabled-protocols: A comma-separated list of SSL protocols to disable. Supported protocols are TLSv1.0, TLSv1.1, and TLSv1.2. If not specified, no protocols are disabled.

  • rate-limit: The maximum rate (in packets per second) at which OpenFlow messages can be sent to the controller. Defaults to 0 (unlimited).

  • burst-limit: The maximum burst size (in packets) for OpenFlow messages. Defaults to 0 (unlimited).

System OPTIONS

System options.

Summary:

options
optional map of string-string pairs
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

options: optional map of string-string pairs

  • datapath-id: The datapath ID of the switch. This is a 64-bit integer that uniquely identifies the switch. If this is unset, then the datapath ID will be auto-generated.

  • hw-addr: The MAC address of the switch. This is used as the datapath ID if the datapath ID is not specified.

  • hw-switch: The type of hardware switch. This is used to configure specific hardware features.

  • hw-mac-table-size: The size of the MAC address table in the hardware switch.

  • hw-flow-table-size: The size of the flow table in the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-ports: The maximum number of ports supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-queues: The maximum number of queues supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-mirrors: The maximum number of mirror ports supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-vlans: The maximum number of VLANs supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-max-rules: The maximum number of rules supported by the hardware switch.

  • hw-table-miss-behavior: The behavior of the hardware switch when a flow is not found in the flow table. Possible values are drop, normal, and controller.

  • hw-table-miss-action: The action to take when a flow is not found in the flow table. This can be a drop, normal, or controller action.

  • hw-flow-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which flow statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-port-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which port statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-queue-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which queue statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-mirror-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which mirror statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-vlan-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which VLAN statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-rule-stats-interval: The interval (in seconds) at which rule statistics are collected from the hardware switch.

  • hw-arp-trap-priority: The priority of ARP trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-nd-trap-priority: The priority of Neighbor Discovery trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-ipv6-ra-trap-priority: The priority of IPv6 Router Advertisement trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-dhcp-trap-priority: The priority of DHCP trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

  • hw-dhcpv6-trap-priority: The priority of DHCPv6 trap packets. Higher values have higher priority.

other_config: optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

IPFIX OPTIONS

IPFIX options.

Summary:

options
optional map of string-string pairs
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

options: optional map of string-string pairs

  • targets: A set of target collector addresses. Each address should be in the format IP:Port.

  • bridge: The name of the bridge to send IPFIX data for. If this is unset, then IPFIX data will be collected for all bridges.

  • cache-entries: The maximum number of IPFIX cache entries. If this is unset, a default value will be used.

  • cache-active-timeout: The interval (in seconds) at which active IPFIX cache entries will be reported. If this is 0, then active entries will only be reported when they are timed out. Default is 300 seconds.

  • cache-inactive-timeout: The interval (in seconds) at which inactive IPFIX cache entries will be reported. If this is 0, then inactive entries will never be reported. Default is 60 seconds.

  • subagent: The IPFIX sub-agent ID. If this is unset, then the sub-agent ID will be auto-generated.

  • obs-domain-id: The IPFIX Observation Domain ID. If this is unset, then the Observation Domain ID will be auto-generated.

  • obs-point-type: The type of observation point. If interface, then the observation point is an interface. If switch, then the observation point is the switch.

  • sampling-rate: The rate at which packets are sampled for IPFIX. For example, a value of 100 means that 1 out of every 100 packets will be sampled.

  • ingress-only: If set to true, only ingress traffic will be sent to the IPFIX collector. Defaults to false.

  • lb-fields: A comma-separated list of fields to use for load balancing IPFIX records across multiple collectors. This is only used when multiple collectors are specified in targets.

other_config: optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

QoS_Update OPTIONS

QoS update options.

Summary:

options
optional map of string-string pairs
Common Columns:
other_config
optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids
optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

options: optional map of string-string pairs

  • qos: The name of the QoS configuration to update.

  • queues: A map from queue ID to queue configuration. The queue configuration struct contains the following fields:

    • min-rate: The minimum rate (in kbps) for this queue.

    • max-rate: The maximum rate (in kbps) for this queue.

    • burst: The burst size (in kbps) for this queue.

other_config: optional map of string-string pairs
external_ids: optional map of string-string pairs

BFD OPTIONS

optional map of string-string pairs

optional map of string-string pairs

Details:

options: optional map of string-string pairs

  • detect-multiplier: The number of missed BFD control packets that will cause BFD to declare the remote endpoint down. Defaults to 3.

  • minimum-tx-interval: The minimum interval (in milliseconds) between transmitted BFD control packets. Defaults to 1000.

  • minimum-rx-interval: The minimum interval (in milliseconds) between received BFD control packets. Defaults to 1000.

  • local-state-change-detector: The BFD state change detector for local ports. Possible values are port-status and packet-count. Defaults to port-status.

    TABLE QoS

    Configuration de la Qualité de Service (QoS) pour chaque Port qui y fait référence.

    Résumé :

    type
    string
    queues
    map d'entiers vers des paires Queue, clé dans la plage 0 à 4 294 967 295
    Configuration pour linux-htb et linux-hfsc :

    other_config : max-rate
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    Configuration pour la QoS egress-policer :

    other_config : cir
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    other_config : cbs
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    other_config : eir
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    other_config : ebs
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    Configuration pour linux-sfq :

    other_config : perturb
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    other_config : quantum
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    Configuration pour linux-netem :

    other_config : latency
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    other_config : limit
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    other_config : loss
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    other_config : jitter
    string optionnel, contenant un entier
    Colonnes communes :

    other_config
    map de string à string
    external_ids
    map de string à string

    Détails :

    type : string
    Le type de QoS à implémenter. Les types actuellement définis sont listés ci-dessous :
    linux-htb
    Classificateur Linux ``hierarchy token bucket’’. Voir tc-htb(8) (également sur http://linux.die.net/man/8/tc-htb) et le manuel HTB (http://luxik.cdi.cz/~devik/qos/htb/manual/userg.htm) pour des informations sur le fonctionnement de ce classificateur et sur la façon de le configurer.
    linux-hfsc
    Classificateur Linux "Hierarchical Fair Service Curve". Voir http://linux-ip.net/articles/hfsc.en/ pour des informations sur le fonctionnement de ce classificateur.
    linux-sfq
    Classificateur Linux ``Stochastic Fairness Queueing’’. Voir tc-sfq(8) (également sur http://linux.die.net/man/8/tc-sfq) pour des informations sur le fonctionnement de ce classificateur.
    linux-codel
    Classificateur Linux ``Controlled Delay’’. Voir tc-codel(8) (également sur http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man8/tc-codel.8.html) pour des informations sur le fonctionnement de ce classificateur.
    linux-fq_codel
    Classificateur Linux ``Fair Queuing with Controlled Delay’’. Voir tc-fq_codel(8) (également sur http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man8/tc-fq_codel.8.html) pour des informations sur le fonctionnement de ce classificateur.
    linux-netem
    Classificateur Linux ``Network Emulator’’. Voir tc-netem(8) (également sur http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man8/tc-netem.8.html) pour des informations sur le fonctionnement de ce classificateur.
    linux-noop
    Classificateur Linux ``Aucune opération.’’ Par défaut, Open vSwitch gère la qualité de service sur tous ses ports configurés. Cela peut être utile, mais parfois les administrateurs préfèrent utiliser d'autres logiciels pour gérer la QoS. Ce type empêche Open vSwitch de modifier la configuration QoS d'un port.
    egress-policer
    Un algorithme de policer de sortie DPDK utilisant la bibliothèque DPDK rte_meter. La bibliothèque rte_meter fournit une implémentation qui permet la mesure et le policage du trafic. L'implémentation dans OVS crée essentiellement un unique bucket de jetons utilisé pour policer le trafic. Il convient de noter que lorsque rte_meter est configuré dans le cadre de la QoS, il y aura un surcoût de performance car rte_meter consommera des cycles CPU pour policer le trafic. Ces cycles CPU sont normalement utilisés pour le traitement des paquets. En tant que tel, la baisse de performance sera remarquée en termes de débit global du trafic.
    trtcm-policer
    Un algorithme de policer de sortie DPDK utilisant le marqueur Two-Rate, Three-Color (TRTCM) de la RFC 4115. C'est un policer hiérarchique à deux niveaux qui effectue d'abord un marquage indépendant de la couleur du trafic au niveau de la file d'attente, suivi d'un marquage dépendant de la couleur au niveau du port. Au final, le trafic marqué comme Vert ou Jaune est transféré, le Rouge est abandonné. Pour plus de détails sur la façon dont le trafic est marqué, voir RFC 4115. Si la ``file d'attente par défaut’’, 0, n'est pas configurée, elle est automatiquement créée avec les mêmes valeurs other_config que le port physique.
    queues : map de entiers vers des paires Queue, clé dans la plage 0 à 4 294 967 295
    Une map des numéros de file d'attente vers des enregistrements Queue. La plage prise en charge des numéros de file d'attente dépend du type. Les numéros de file d'attente sont les mêmes que le queue_id utilisé dans OpenFlow dans struct ofp_action_enqueue et d'autres structures.
    La file d'attente 0 est la ``file d'attente par défaut.’’ Elle est utilisée par les actions de sortie OpenFlow lorsqu'aucune file d'attente spécifique n'a été définie. Lorsqu'aucune configuration pour la file d'attente 0 n'est présente, elle est automatiquement configurée comme si un enregistrement Queue avec des colonnes dscp et other_config vides avait été spécifié. (Avant la version 1.6, Open vSwitch laissait la file d'attente 0 non configurée dans ce cas. Avec certaines disciplines de mise en file d'attente, cela abandonnait tous les paquets destinés à la file d'attente par défaut.)
    Configuration pour linux-htb et linux-hfsc :

    Les classes linux-htb et linux-hfsc prennent en charge la paire clé-valeur suivante :

    other_config : max-rate : string optionnel, contenant un entier
    Débit maximum partagé par tout le trafic mis en file d'attente, en bit/s. Optionnel. Si non spécifié, pour les interfaces physiques, la valeur par défaut est le débit du lien. Pour d'autres interfaces ou si le débit du lien ne peut pas être déterminé, la valeur par défaut est actuellement de 10 Gbps.
    Configuration pour la QoS egress-policer :

    Le type de QoS egress-policer fournit un policage de sortie pour les types de ports userspace avec DPDK. Il possède les paires clé-valeur suivantes.

    other_config : cir : string optionnel, contenant un entier
    Le Committed Information Rate (CIR) est mesuré en octets de paquets IP par seconde, c'est-à-dire qu'il inclut l'en-tête IP, mais pas les en-têtes spécifiques à la liaison (par exemple, Ethernet). Cela représente le débit d'octets par seconde auquel le bucket de jetons sera mis à jour. La valeur cir est calculée par (pps x taille des données du paquet). Par exemple, en supposant qu'un utilisateur souhaite limiter un flux composé de paquets de 64 octets à 1 million de paquets par seconde, le CIR serait défini sur 46000000. Cette valeur peut être décomposée en ``1 000 000 x 46’’. Où 1 000 000 est le débit de policage pour le nombre de paquets par seconde et 46 représente la taille des données du paquet pour un paquet IP de 64 octets sans 14 octets d'en-tête Ethernet et 4 octets de FCS.
    other_config : cbs : string optionnel, contenant un entier
    Le Committed Burst Size (CBS) est mesuré en octets et représente un bucket de jetons. Au minimum, cette valeur doit être définie sur la taille attendue du plus gros paquet dans le flux de trafic. En pratique, des valeurs plus importantes peuvent être utilisées pour augmenter la taille du bucket de jetons. Si un paquet peut être transmis, le cbs sera décrémenté du nombre d'octets/jetons du paquet. S'il n'y a pas assez de jetons dans le bucket cbs, le paquet sera abandonné.
    other_config : eir : string optionnel, contenant un entier
    Le

    QoS

    Quality of Service configuration. The QoS table is used to configure network traffic shaping and policing. It is used in conjunction with a Port.

    Summary:

    type
    optional string, one of linux-htb, linux-hfsc, linux-sfq, linux-netem, windows-dqos
    Configuration for linux-htb, linux-hfsc, linux-sfq, linux-netem QoS:
    other_config : eir
    optional string, containing an integer
    other_config : ebs
    optional string, containing an integer
    Common Columns:
    other_config
    map of string-string pairs
    external_ids
    map of string-string pairs

    Details:

    A QoS record configures Quality of Service settings on a port. The specific options available depend on the type specified.

    Configuration for linux-htb QoS:

    The linux-htb QoS supports the following key-value pairs:

    other_config : min-rate: optional string, containing an integer
    Minimum guaranteed bandwidth, in bit/s.
    other_config : max-rate: optional string, containing an integer
    Maximum allowed bandwidth, in bit/s. Optional. If specified, the queue's rate will not be allowed to exceed the specified value, even if excess bandwidth is available. If unspecified, defaults to no limit.
    other_config : burst: optional string, containing an integer
    Burst size, in bits. This is the maximum amount of ``credits’’ that a queue can accumulate while it is idle. Optional. Details of the linux-htb implementation require a minimum burst size, so a too-small burst will be silently ignored.
    other_config : priority: optional string, containing an integer
    A queue with a smaller priority will receive all the excess bandwidth that it can use before a queue with a larger value receives any. Specific priority values are unimportant; only relative ordering matters. Defaults to 0 if unspecified.
    Configuration for linux-hfsc QoS:

    The linux-hfsc QoS supports the following key-value pairs:

    other_config : min-rate: optional string, containing an integer
    Minimum guaranteed bandwidth, in bit/s.
    other_config : max-rate: optional string, containing an integer
    Maximum allowed bandwidth, in bit/s. Optional. If specified, the queue's rate will not be allowed to exceed the specified value, even if excess bandwidth is available. If unspecified, defaults to no limit.
    Configuration for linux-sfq QoS:

    The linux-sfq QoS supports the following key-value pairs:

    other_config : eir: optional string, containing an integer
    Excess Information Rate (EIR) is measured in bytes of IP packets per second, i.e. it includes the IP header, but not link specific (e.g. Ethernet) headers. This represents the bytes per second rate at which the token bucket will be updated. The eir value is calculated by (pps x packet data size). For example assuming a user wishes to limit a stream consisting of 64 byte packets to 1 million packets per second the EIR would be set to to to 46000000. This value can be broken into ’1,000,000 x 46’. Where 1,000,000 is the policing rate for the number of packets per second and 46 represents the size of the packet data for a 64 bytes IP packet without 14 bytes Ethernet and 4 bytes FCS header.
    other_config : ebs: optional string, containing an integer
    The Excess Burst Size (EBS) is measured in bytes and represents a token bucket. At a minimum this value should be be set to the expected largest size packet in the traffic stream. In practice larger values may be used to increase the size of the token bucket. If a packet can be transmitted then the ebs will be decremented by the number of bytes/tokens of the packet. If there are not enough tokens in the cbs bucket the packet might be dropped.
    Configuration for linux-sfq:

    The linux-sfq QoS supports the following key-value pairs:

    other_config : perturb: optional string, containing an integer
    Number of seconds between consecutive perturbations in hashing algorithm. Different flows can end up in the same hash bucket causing unfairness. Perturbation’s goal is to remove possible unfairness. The default and recommended value is 10. Too low a value is discouraged because each perturbation can cause packet reordering.
    other_config : quantum: optional string, containing an integer
    Number of bytes linux-sfq QoS can dequeue in one turn in round-robin from one flow. The default and recommended value is equal to interface’s MTU.
    Configuration for linux-netem:

    The linux-netem QoS supports the following key-value pairs:

    other_config : latency: optional string, containing an integer
    Adds the chosen delay to the packets outgoing to chosen network interface. The latency value expressed in us.
    other_config : limit: optional string, containing an integer
    Maximum number of packets the qdisc may hold queued at a time. The default value is 1000.
    other_config : loss: optional string, containing an integer
    Adds an independent loss probability to the packets outgoing from the chosen network interface.
    other_config : jitter: optional string, containing an integer
    Adds the provided jitter to the latency outgoing to the chosen network interface. The jitter value expressed in us.
    Common Columns:

    The overall purpose of these columns is described under Common Columns at the beginning of this document.

    other_config: map of string-string pairs
    external_ids: map of string-string pairs

    dscp: optional integer, in range 0 to 63
    If set, Open vSwitch will mark all traffic egressing this Queue with the given DSCP bits. Traffic egressing the default Queue is only marked if it was explicitly selected as the Queue at the time the packet was output. If unset, the DSCP bits of traffic egressing this Queue will remain unchanged.
    Configuration for linux-htb QoS:

    other_config : max-rate: optional string, containing an integer, at least 1
    Maximum allowed bandwidth, in bit/s. Optional. If specified, the queue’s rate will not be allowed to exceed the specified value, even if excess bandwidth is available. If unspecified, defaults to no limit.
    other_config : burst: optional string, containing an integer, at least 1
    Burst size, in bits. This is the maximum amount of ``credits’’ that a queue can accumulate while it is idle. Optional. Details of the linux-htb implementation require a minimum burst size, so a too-small burst will be silently ignored.
    other_config : priority: optional string, containing an integer, in range 0 to 4,294,967,295
    A queue with a smaller priority will receive all the excess bandwidth that it can use before a queue with a larger value receives any. Specific priority values are unimportant; only relative ordering matters. Defaults to 0 if unspecified.
    Configuration for linux-hfsc QoS:

    other_config : min-rate: optional string, containing an integer, at least 1

    other_config : max-rate: optional string, containing an integer, at least 1
    Maximum allowed bandwidth, in bit/s. Optional. If specified, the queue’s rate will not be allowed to exceed the specified value, even if excess bandwidth is available. If unspecified, defaults to no limit.
    Common Columns:

    The overall purpose of these columns is described under Common Columns at the beginning of this document.

    external_ids: map of string-string pairs

    Mirror TABLE

    A port mirror within a Bridge.

    A port mirror configures a bridge to send selected frames to special ``mirrored’’ ports, in addition to their normal destinations. Mirroring traffic may also be referred to as SPAN or RSPAN, depending on how the mirrored traffic is sent.

    When a packet enters an Open vSwitch bridge, it becomes eligible for mirroring based on its ingress port and VLAN. As the packet travels through the flow tables, each time it is output to a port, it becomes eligible for mirroring based on the egress port and VLAN. In Open vSwitch 2.5 and later, mirroring occurs just after a packet first becomes eligible, using the packet as it exists at that point; in Open vSwitch 2.4 and earlier, mirroring occurs only after a packet has traversed all the flow tables, using the original packet as it entered the bridge. This makes a difference only when the flow table modifies the packet: in Open vSwitch 2.4, the modifications are never visible to mirrors, whereas in Open vSwitch 2.5 and later modifications made before the first output that makes i

    t éligibles pour la mise en miroir vers une destination particulière sont visibles.

    Un paquet qui entre dans un pont Open vSwitch est mis en miroir vers une destination particulière une seule fois, même s'il est éligible pour plusieurs raisons. Par exemple, un paquet serait mis en miroir vers un output_port particulier une seule fois, même s'il est sélectionné pour la mise en miroir vers ce port par select_dst_port et select_src_port dans des enregistrements Mirror identiques ou différents.

    Résumé :

    name
    chaîne de caractères
    Sélection des paquets pour la mise en miroir :

    select_all
    booléen
    select_dst_port
    ensemble de références faibles à des Ports
    select_src_port
    ensemble de références faibles à des Ports
    select_vlan
    ensemble de 4 096 entiers au maximum, dans la plage 0 à 4 095
    filter
    chaîne de caractères optionnelle
    Configuration de la destination de mise en miroir :

    output_port
    référence faible optionnelle à un Port
    output_vlan
    entier optionnel, dans la plage 1 à 4 095
    snaplen
    entier optionnel, dans la plage 14 à 65 535
    Statistiques : Compteurs de miroir :

    statistics : tx_packets
    entier optionnel
    statistics : tx_bytes
    entier optionnel
    Colonnes communes :

    external_ids
    paire de chaînes de caractères

    Détails :

    name: chaîne de caractères
    Identifiant arbitraire pour le Mirror.
    Sélection des paquets pour la mise en miroir :

    Pour être sélectionné pour la mise en miroir, un paquet donné doit entrer ou sortir du pont via un port sélectionné et il doit également être dans l'un des VLANs sélectionnés.

    select_all: booléen
    Si vrai, tous les paquets arrivant ou partant sur n'importe quel port sont sélectionnés pour la mise en miroir.
    select_dst_port: ensemble de références faibles à des Ports
    Ports sur lesquels les paquets partants sont sélectionnés pour la mise en miroir.
    select_src_port: ensemble de références faibles à des Ports
    Ports sur lesquels les paquets arrivants sont sélectionnés pour la mise en miroir.
    select_vlan: ensemble de 4 096 entiers au maximum, dans la plage 0 à 4 095
    VLANs sur lesquels les paquets sont sélectionnés pour la mise en miroir. Un ensemble vide sélectionne les paquets sur tous les VLANs.
    filter: chaîne de caractères optionnelle
    Lorsqu'il est défini, seuls les paquets correspondant au filter sont sélectionnés pour la mise en miroir. Les paquets qui ne correspondent pas sont ignorés par ce miroir. La syntaxe du filter est décrite dans ovs-fields(7). Cependant, le champ in_port n'est pas pris en charge ; select_src_port doit être utilisé pour limiter le miroir à un port source.
    Ce filtre est appliqué après select_all, select_dst_port, select_src_port et select_vlan.
    Configuration de la destination de mise en miroir :

    Ces colonnes sont mutuellement exclusives. L'une d'entre elles doit être non vide.

    output_port: référence faible optionnelle à un Port
    Port de sortie pour les paquets sélectionnés, s'il n'est pas vide.
    La spécification d'un port pour la sortie miroir réserve ce port exclusivement pour la mise en miroir. Aucune trame autre que celles sélectionnées pour la mise en miroir via cette colonne ne sera transmise au port, et toutes les trames reçues sur le port seront supprimées.
    Le port de sortie peut être de n'importe quel type de port pris en charge par Open vSwitch. Il peut s'agir, par exemple, d'un port physique (parfois appelé SPAN) ou d'un tunnel GRE.
    output_vlan: entier optionnel, dans la plage 1 à 4 095
    VLAN de sortie pour les paquets sélectionnés, s'il n'est pas vide.
    Les trames seront envoyées sur tous les ports qui font un trunk du output_vlan, ainsi que sur les ports ayant un VLAN implicite output_vlan. Lorsqu'une trame mise en miroir est envoyée sur un port de trunk, la balise VLAN de la trame sera définie sur output_vlan, remplaçant toute balise existante ; lorsqu'elle est envoyée sur un port VLAN implicite, la trame ne sera pas balisée. Ce type de mise en miroir est parfois appelé RSPAN.
    Voir la documentation de other_config:forward-bpdu dans la table Interface pour une liste des adresses MAC de destination qui ne seront pas mises en miroir vers un VLAN afin d'éviter de confondre les commutateurs qui interprètent les protocoles qu'elles représentent.
    Veuillez noter : La mise en miroir vers un VLAN peut perturber un réseau contenant des commutateurs non gérés. Considérons un commutateur physique non géré avec deux ports : port 1, connecté à un hôte final, et port 2, connecté à un Open vSwitch configuré pour mettre en miroir les paquets reçus dans le VLAN 123 sur le port 2. Supposons que l'hôte final envoie un paquet sur le port 1 que le commutateur physique transmet au port 2. L'Open vSwitch transmet ce paquet à sa destination puis le renvoie sur le port 2 dans le VLAN 123. Ce paquet réfléchi amène le commutateur physique non géré à remplacer l'entrée de la table d'apprentissage MAC, qui pointait correctement vers le port 1, par une entrée qui pointe incorrectement vers le port 2. Par la suite, le commutateur physique dirigera les paquets destinés à l'hôte final vers l'Open vSwitch sur le port 2, au lieu de l'hôte final sur le port 1, perturbant ainsi la connectivité. Si la mise en miroir vers un VLAN est souhaitée dans ce scénario, alors le commutateur physique doit être remplacé par un commutateur qui apprend les adresses Ethernet sur une base par VLAN. De plus, l'apprentissage doit être désactivé sur le VLAN contenant le trafic mis en miroir. Si cela n'est pas fait, les commutateurs intermédiaires apprendront l'adresse MAC de chaque hôte final à partir du trafic mis en miroir. Si les paquets envoyés à cet hôte final sont également mis en miroir, ils seront rejetés car le commutateur tentera de les envoyer sur le port d'entrée. La désactivation de l'apprentissage pour le VLAN amènera le commutateur à envoyer correctement le paquet sur tous les ports configurés pour ce VLAN. Si Open vSwitch est utilisé comme commutateur intermédiaire, l'apprentissage peut être désactivé en ajoutant le VLAN mis en miroir à flood_vlans dans la ou les tables Bridge appropriées.
    La mise en miroir vers un tunnel GRE a moins d'inconvénients que la mise en miroir vers un VLAN et devrait généralement être préférée.
    snaplen: entier optionnel, dans la plage 14 à 65 535
    Nombre maximal d'octets par paquet à mettre en miroir.
    Un paquet mis en miroir dont la taille est supérieure à snaplen sera tronqué dans le plan de données à snaplen octets avant d'être envoyé au port de sortie du miroir. Si omis, les paquets ne sont pas tronqués.
    Statistiques : Compteurs de miroir :

    Paires clé-valeur qui rapportent les statistiques de miroir. La période de mise à jour est contrôlée par other_config:stats-update-interval dans la table Open_vSwitch.

    statistics : tx_packets: entier optionnel
    Nombre de paquets transmis via ce miroir.
    statistics : tx_bytes: entier optionnel
    Nombre d'octets transmis via ce miroir.
    Colonnes communes :

    L'objectif général de ces colonnes est décrit sous Colonnes communes au début de ce document.

    external_ids: paire de chaînes de caractères
